[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

      end

            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe

               .rst        (rst),

               .en         (en),

               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),
               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));
         end
      end
   endgenerate

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

[Step 2/60]
// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

  -> create_file(filename='design.v', content="// Area-optimized weight-stationary systolic matmul tile (TPU MXU clone).\n//\n// Same cycle behavior as the logikbench tpu but the internal structure is\n// levelized: activations are captured directly in the PE register row above\n// the weight they meet (no separate skew SRs), each column keeps its own\n// accumulator, and the weight load path is a broadcast (no column shift\n// chains). Only the small output de-skew (max 7 stages of 32b) and the valid\n// pipeline are retained, together with a 3-cycle weight load enable counter.\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n    );\n\n   // the array advances only while w_valid is low\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage. Broadcast load: on the k-th w_valid cycle the bottom\n   // matrix row B[7-k][*] is presented, so with a 3..0 down-counter the value\n   // written into row r is w_data of cycle 7-r, exactly as if the rows had\n   // been shifted south for 8 cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n\n   reg [2:0] wcnt;   // 8 load cycles -> counts 7..0 during w_valid\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         wcnt <= 3'd7;\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (w_valid)\n           wcnt <= (wcnt == 3'd0) ? 3'd7 : wcnt - 3'd1;\n         else\n           wcnt <= 3'd7;   // rearm so a later reload starts from row 7\n      end\n   end\n\n   integer wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1)\n                   w[wcnt][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n      end\n   end\n   // (weights need no reset: they are always fully loaded before compute)\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation registers. a[i] is the value entering column 0 of row i on\n   // THIS cycle (i.e. it meets the weight of row i this cycle); the original\n   // injects row i i cycles late, so this slot carries A[m][i]. The array\n   // input itself is that value delayed one more cycle, which is exactly the\n   // original PE a_east pipeline register.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] a_in [0:N-1];      // value meeting row i's weight this cycle\n   reg [DW-1:0] a_arr [0:N-1];     // arrival registers (original column 0)\n   integer      ai;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (ai = 0; ai < N; ai = ai + 1) begin\n            a_in[ai]  <= a_valid ? a_data[ai*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            a_arr[ai] <= a_in[ai];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   // activation entering column j of row i during compute (combinational)\n   wire [DW-1:0] ain [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   genvar\t       gi, gj;\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_ain\n         assign ain[gi][0] = a_arr[gi];\n         for (gj = 1; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_ainc\n            assign ain[gi][gj] = (gj == gi + 1) ? a_in[gi] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Column accumulators: col[j] += sum_i A[m][i]*B[i][j] for the row being\n   // consumed this cycle. Pairs add nothing, so only the diagonal terms feed\n   // the adder tree. A zero row (a_valid low on that arrival) leaves the\n   // accumulator unchanged, matching idle/drain cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg signed [ACCW-1:0] col [0:N-1];\n   wire signed [ACCW-1:0] nxt [0:N-1];\n\n   generate\n      for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_col\n         wire signed [2*DW-1:0] pr [0:N-1];\n         for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_pr\n            assign pr[gi] = $signed(ain[gi][gj]) * $signed(w[gi][gj]);\n         end\n         wire signed [ACCW-1:0] psum =\n                   $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[0][2*DW-1]}}, pr[0]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[1][2*DW-1]}}, pr[1]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[2][2*DW-1]}}, pr[2]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[3][2*DW-1]}}, pr[3]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[4][2*DW-1]}}, pr[4]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[5][2*DW-1]}}, pr[5]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[6][2*DW-1]}}, pr[6]})\n                 + $signed({{(ACCW-2*DW){pr[7][2*DW-1]}}, pr[7]});\n         assign nxt[gj] = col[gj] + psum;\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   integer cj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         for (cj = 0; cj < N; cj = cj + 1)\n                   col[cj] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else if (en)\n        for (cj = 0; cj < N; cj = cj + 1)\n                   col[cj] <= nxt[cj];\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j compute cycles so all columns\n   // of a result row leave together. Column N-1 (j=7) needs no delay, so\n   // register only columns 0..6.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg signed [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:N-2][0:N-2];   // dr[j][k], j=0..N-2\n   genvar\t       dj, dk;\n   generate\n      for (dj = 0; dj < N-1; dj = dj + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - dj;\n         if (D == 1) begin : g_d1\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) dr[dj][0] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n               else if (en) dr[dj][0] <= col[dj];\n            end\n         end\n         else begin : g_dn\n            for (dk = 0; dk < D; dk = dk + 1) begin : g_dsr\n               always @(posedge clk) begin\n                  if (rst) dr[dj][dk] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n                  else if (en) dr[dj][dk] <= (dk == 0) ? col[dj] : dr[dj][dk-1];\n               end\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   genvar oj;\n   generate\n      for (oj = 0; oj < N; oj = oj + 1) begin : g_out\n         if (oj == N-1) begin : g_o7\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] = col[N-1];\n         end\n         else begin : g_on\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[oj][N-2-oj];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n")

Now let me analyze the timing carefully. The key insight: results are only checked when `c_valid` is high (exactly 15 cycles after each accepted A row), so I can restructure the internals freely as long as outputs align. Let me try a "delay line + parallel per-column dot-product tree" design (much cheaper than 64 PEs with 32-bit psum chains):
  -> create_file(content="// Area-optimized TPU MXU clone.\n//\n// Instead of 64 PEs with 32-bit partial-sum chains + input skew + output\n// de-skew, the activation row is simply delayed 15 cycles in an 8-bit-wide\n// delay line (the narrowest place to absorb the mandatory latency) and the\n// whole dot product per column is computed in parallel with an adder tree.\n// Weights keep the exact shift-south load semantics of the original.\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n    );\n\n   // the array advances only while w_valid is low\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: shift south on every w_valid cycle (identical semantics\n   // to the original per-column shift register chain).\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1)\n           for (wi = 0; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n             w[wi][wj] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1) begin\n            w[0][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n            for (wi = 1; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n              w[wi][wj] <= w[wi-1][wj];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation delay: row m is captured at the end of cycle m (zero when\n   // a_valid is low) and held for 15 cycles so that it is present at the\n   // multiplier inputs during cycle m+15, exactly when its result must be\n   // presented on c_data.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] dly [0:N-1][0:2*N-2];\n   integer       di, dk;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (di = 0; di < N; di = di + 1) begin\n            dly[di][0] <= a_valid ? a_data[di*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            for (dk = 1; dk < 2*N-1; dk = dk + 1)\n              dly[di][dk] <= dly[di][dk-1];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   wire [DW-1:0] e [0:N-1];\n   genvar\t       gi;\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_e\n         assign e[gi] = dly[gi][2*N-2];\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // 64 fixed multipliers + one 8-input adder tree per column.\n   //##########################################################################\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] p [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   genvar\t\t       pi, pj;\n   generate\n      for (pi = 0; pi < N; pi = pi + 1) begin : g_prow\n         for (pj = 0; pj < N; pj = pj + 1) begin : g_pcol\n            assign p[pi][pj] = $signed(e[pi]) * $signed(w[pi][pj]);\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire signed [19:0] t3 [0:N-1];\n   generate\n      for (pj = 0; pj < N; pj = pj + 1) begin : g_tree\n         wire signed [19:0] t1 [0:3];\n         wire signed [19:0] t2 [0:1];\n         assign t1[0] = p[0][pj] + p[1][pj];\n         assign t1[1] = p[2][pj] + p[3][pj];\n         assign t1[2] = p[4][pj] + p[5][pj];\n         assign t1[3] = p[6][pj] + p[7][pj];\n         assign t2[0] = t1[0] + t1[1];\n         assign t2[1] = t1[2] + t1[3];\n         assign t3[pj] = t2[0] + t2[1];\n         assign c_data[pj*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-20){t3[pj][19]}}, t3[pj]};\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed 15 compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design2.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now I understand the key subtlety: products must be computed at their **exact original cycles** (weight registers change during loads, so in-flight rows mix old/new weights diagonally). So I'll keep the systolic timing but aggressively narrow the datapath (tapered psum widths: results need only 19 bits, not 32) and merge skew+flow registers.
  -> create_file(content="// Area-optimized weight-stationary systolic matmul tile (TPU MXU clone).\n//\n// Cycle-exact clone of the logikbench tpu, restructured for area:\n//  * per-row activation skew and the east flow are one shift register chain\n//    (row i needs stages 1..i+7; the tap for column j is stage i+j), which is\n//    the same register count as the PE a_east pipeline but without duplicate\n//    edge logic,\n//  * the partial-sum chain is width-tapered: after k products the value needs\n//    at most 16..19 bits (|sum| <= k*16384), so the 32-bit accumulators of the\n//    original shrink to 16/17/17/18/18/18/18/19 bits down each column,\n//  * the output de-skew and c_data are 19-bit, sign-extended at the pins,\n//  * no resets on the datapath (it is fully self-flushing: the psum chain is\n//    seeded from a constant zero and the activation chain is flushed by the\n//    zeros injected while a_valid is low); only the valid pipe is reset.\n// Products are still formed at exactly the original cycles with the weight\n// value current at that cycle, so mid-drain weight reloads behave identically.\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n    );\n\n   // the array advances only while w_valid is low\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: per-column south shift while w_valid (original semantics,\n   // including partial shifts).\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1) begin\n            w[0][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n            for (wi = 1; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n              w[wi][wj] <= w[wi-1][wj];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation skew + east flow. sr[i][k] is delay stage k+1 of array row i;\n   // the multiplier input of column j is stage i+j (combinational input for\n   // i=j=0). Row i needs stages 1..i+7.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:N-1][0:N+5];\n   integer       si, sk;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (si = 0; si < N; si = si + 1) begin\n            sr[si][0] <= a_valid ? a_data[si*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            for (sk = 1; sk <= si+6; sk = sk + 1)\n              sr[si][sk] <= sr[si][sk-1];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   wire [DW-1:0] tap [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   genvar\t       gi, gj;\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_taprow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_tapcol\n            if (gi == 0 && gj == 0) begin : g_c00\n               assign tap[0][0] = a_valid ? a_data[0 +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            end\n            else begin : g_cnn\n               assign tap[gi][gj] = sr[gi][gi+gj-1];\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Products: formed at exactly the original cycles with the weight current\n   // at that cycle.\n   //##########################################################################\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prd [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_prrow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_prcol\n            assign prd[gi][gj] = $signed(tap[gi][gj]) * $signed(w[gi][gj]);\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Partial-sum chain, width-tapered down each column.\n   //##########################################################################\n   wire signed [18:0] psw [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   generate\n      for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_pscol\n         for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_psrow\n            localparam integer W =\n\t\t\t       (gi==0) ? 16 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==1) ? 17 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==2) ? 17 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==3) ? 18 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==4) ? 18 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==5) ? 18 :\n\t\t\t       (gi==6) ? 18 : 19;\n            reg signed [W-1:0] ps;\n            if (gi == 0) begin : g_r0\n               always @(posedge clk) begin\n                  if (en) ps <= prd[0][gj];\n               end\n            end\n            else begin : g_rn\n               wire signed [W-1:0] prev = psw[gi-1][gj][W-1:0];\n               wire signed [W-1:0] pext = {{(W-16){prd[gi][gj][15]}}, prd[gi][gj]};\n               always @(posedge clk) begin\n                  if (en) ps <= prev + pext;\n               end\n            end\n            assign psw[gi][gj] = {{(19-W){ps[W-1]}}, ps};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg signed [18:0] dsr [0:N-2][0:N-2];\n   genvar\t\tdj, dk;\n   generate\n      for (dj = 0; dj < N-1; dj = dj + 1) begin : g_dcol\n         localparam integer D = N-1-dj;\n         for (dk = 0; dk < D; dk = dk + 1) begin : g_dstage\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en)\n                 dsr[dj][dk] <= (dk == 0) ? psw[N-1][dj] : dsr[dj][dk-1];\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   genvar oj;\n   generate\n      for (oj = 0; oj < N; oj = oj + 1) begin : g_out\n         if (oj == N-1) begin : g_last\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n\t      {{(ACCW-19){psw[N-1][N-1][18]}}, psw[N-1][N-1]};\n         end\n         else begin : g_oth\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n\t      {{(ACCW-19){dsr[oj][N-2-oj][18]}}, dsr[oj][N-2-oj]};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed 2N-1 compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design3.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Design3 passes at 3.17M (down from 4.62M). Now the critical path is FF â multiplier â carry-propagate add â FF. Let me restructure the partial-sum chain into **carry-save form** (3:2 compressors down the column, one final CPA at the bottom), which removes the per-cell carry-propagate add from the multiplier's path:
  -> create_file(content="// Area/delay-optimized weight-stationary systolic matmul tile (TPU MXU clone).\n//\n// Cycle-exact clone of the logikbench tpu. Structure:\n//  * per-row activation skew + east flow = one shift register chain per row,\n//  * products formed at exactly the original cycles with the weight value\n//    current at that cycle (so mid-drain weight reloads behave identically),\n//  * the partial-sum chain down each column is kept in CARRY-SAVE form\n//    (one 3:2 compressor per cell) so the multiplier output only drives a\n//    compressor, not a carry-propagate adder; a single CPA at the bottom of\n//    each column produces the final 19-bit result,\n//  * width-tapered carry-save stages, 19-bit output de-skew,\n//  * no datapath resets (self-flushing); only the valid pipe is reset.\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n   );\n\n   // the array advances only while w_valid is low\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: per-column south shift while w_valid.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1) begin\n            w[0][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n            for (wi = 1; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n              w[wi][wj] <= w[wi-1][wj];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation skew + east flow. sr[i][k] is delay stage k+1 of row i; the\n   // multiplier tap of column j is stage i+j (combinational input for i=j=0).\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:N-1][0:N+5];\n   integer       si, sk;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (si = 0; si < N; si = si + 1) begin\n            sr[si][0] <= a_valid ? a_data[si*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            for (sk = 1; sk <= si+6; sk = sk + 1)\n              sr[si][sk] <= sr[si][sk-1];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   wire [DW-1:0] tap [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   genvar\t       gi, gj;\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_prrow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_prcol\n            assign prd[gi][gj] = $signed(tap[gi][gj]) * $signed(w[gi][gj]);\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Carry-save partial-sum chain down each column.\n   //   value of row i  = S[i] (signed, 16+i bits) + C[i] (unsigned, 17+i bits)\n   //   row 0 : S[0] = product, C[0] = 0\n   //   row i : (S[i], C[i]) = 3:2 compress( S[i-1], C[i-1], prd[i] )\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [22:0] S [0:N-1]; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_row\n            localparam integer W = 16 + gi;\n            wire [W-1:0] a = {S[gi-1][W-2], S[gi-1][W-2:0]};\n            wire [W-1:0] b = C[gi-1][W-1:0];\n            wire [W-1:0] c = {{(W-16){prd[gi][gj][15]}}, prd[gi][gj]};\n            wire [W-1:0] snew = a ^ b ^ c;\n            wire [W:0]   cnew = {((a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c)), 1'b0};\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  S[gi] <= {{(23-W){snew[W-1]}}, snew};\n                  C[gi] <= {{(23-W){1'b0}}, cnew};\n               end\n            end\n         end\n         // final carry-propagate add at the bottom of the column\n         wire [23:0]     tot = S[N-1] + C[N-1];\n         wire signed [18:0] res = tot[18:0];\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg signed [18:0] dsr [0:N-2][0:N-2];\n   genvar\t\tdj, dk;\n   generate\n      for (dj = 0;   -> create_file(content="// Optimized weight-stationary systolic matmul tile (TPU MXU clone).\n// Same cycle behavior as the logikbench tpu; structure: per-row activation\n// skew/flow shift register, products at the original cycles, carry-save\n// partial-sum chain down each column (3:2 compressors), one CPA per column,\n// and a single-chain output de-skew (newest result enters at the bottom of\n// the chain, oldest leaves at the top), which needs only 6 registers per\n// column instead of 28.\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n   );\n\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: per-column south shift while w_valid.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1) begin\n            w[0][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n            for (wi = 1; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n              w[wi][wj] <= w[wi-1][wj];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation skew + east flow: sr[i][k] is delay stage k+1 of row i; the\n   // multiplier tap of column j is stage i+j (combinational for i=j=0).\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:N-1][0:N+5];\n   integer       si, sk;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (si = 0; si < N; si = si + 1) begin\n            sr[si][0] <= a_valid ? a_data[si*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            for (sk = 1; sk <= si+6; sk = sk + 1)\n              sr[si][sk] <= sr[si][sk-1];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   wire [DW-1:0] tap [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   genvar\t       gi, gj;\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_taprow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_tapcol\n            if (gi == 0 && gj == 0) begin : g_c00\n               assign tap[0][0] = a_valid ? a_data[0 +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            end\n            else begin : g_cnn\n               assign tap[gi][gj] = sr[gi][gi+gj-1];\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Products (formed at the original cycles, with the current weights).\n   //##########################################################################\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prd [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   generate\n      for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_prrow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_prcol\n            assign prd[gi][gj] = $signed(tap[gi][gj]) * $signed(w[gi][gj]);\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Carry-save partial-sum chain down each column (fixed 19-bit modulus):\n   //   value = S + C,  (S[i], C[i]) = 3:2(S[i-1], C[i-1], prd[i])\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [18:0] S [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   reg [18:0] C [1:N-1][0:N-1];\n   wire [18:0] colres [0:N-1];\n\n   generate\n      for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_col\n         always @(posedge clk) begin\n            if (en)\n              S[0][gj] <= {{3{prd[0][gj][15]}}, prd[0][gj]};\n         end\n         for (gi = 1; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_row\n            wire [18:0] a = S[gi-1][gj];\n            wire [18:0] b = (gi > 1) ? C[gi-1][gj] : {19{1'b0}};\n            wire [18:0] c = {{3{prd[gi][gj][15]}}, prd[gi][gj]};\n            wire [18:0] snew = a ^ b ^ c;\n            wire [18:0] cnew = {((a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c)), 1'b0};\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) begin\n                  S[gi][gj] <= snew;\n                  C[gi][gj] <= cnew;\n               end\n            end\n         end\n         assign colres[gj] = S[N-1][gj] + C[N-1][gj];\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Output de-skew: column j needs N-1-j extra cycles. One chain per column,\n   // newest result enters the bottom, the oldest leaves at the top after the\n   // required number of cycles. Column 7 (no delay) is just registered.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [18:0] dq [0:N-2][0:N-2];   // dq[j][0] = newest, up the chain\n   integer     qi, qj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (qj = 0; qj < N-1; qj = qj + 1) begin\n            dq[qj][0] <= colres[qj];\n            for (qi = 1; qi < N-1; qi = qi + 1)\n              dq[qj][qi] <= dq[qj][qi-1];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   genvar oj;\n   generate\n      for (oj = 0; oj < N; oj = oj + 1) begin : g_out\n         if (oj == N-1) begin : g_last\n            reg [18:0] lat;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en) lat <= colres[N-1];\n            end\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n\t      {{(ACCW-19){lat[18]}}, lat};\n         end\n         else begin : g_oth\n            assign c_data[oj*ACCW +: ACCW] =\n\t      {{(ACCW-19){dq[oj][N-2-oj][18]}}, dq[oj][N-2-oj]};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed 2N-1 compute cycles.\n   //##########################################################################\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design5.v')

After k products: |value| ≤ k·16384. Signed bits needed: k=1: 16384 needs 16 bits (range [-16384, 16383] → 16 bits: -16384 = 0x8000 ✓). k=2: 32768 → 17 bits. k=3: 49152 → 17 bits (2^16 = 65536 > 49152, and signed 17 bits covers ±65536... 17 bits signed covers [-65536, 65535] ✓ so 17 bits). Stage i computes: (S_i, C_i) = 3:2(S_{i-1}, C_{i-1}, prd_i) with all operands W_i bits, and the invariant: S_i + C_i ≡ V_i (mod 2^{W_i}) where V_i = sum of products so far. If W_{i-1} < W_i, then S_{i-1} + C_{i-1} ≡ V_{i-1} (mod 2^{W_{i-1}}), i.e., S_{i-1} + C_{i-1} = V_{i-1} + m·2^{W_{i-1}} for some integer m ≥ 0 (since 0 ≤ S+C < 2^{W_{i-1}+1}, m ∈ {0,1}).

If we widen S_{i-1} by sign-extension (replicating its top bit) and C_{i-1} by zero-extension, then the widened sum = (S_{i-1} + C_{i-1}) + sign_correction where sign_correction = (S_top extended) contribution... Let me be concrete: S' = S + s·(2^{W_i} - 2^{W_{i-1}}) where s = S's top bit (0 or 1) — because sign-extending a W_{i-1}-bit two's complement number to W_i bits adds s·(2^{W_i} - 2^{W_{i-1}}). Similarly C' = C (zero-extension adds nothing). So S' + C' = V_{i-1} + m·2^{W_{i-1}} + s·(2^{W_i} - 2^{W_{i-1}}).

Then (S_i, C_i) = 3:2(S', C', prd') where prd' = sign-extended product. The 3:2 gives S_i + C_i = S' + C' + prd' EXACTLY (as integers, since the 3:2 compressor computes the exact sum of three W_i-bit numbers as a W_i-bit sum plus a carry out... hmm, careful: the 3:2 compressor computes s = a^b^c and carries = (a&b)|(a&c)|(b&c) shifted left 1. Then s + carries = a + b + c EXACTLY as integers if there's no overflow beyond W_i+1 bits. Actually a+b+c ≤ 3·(2^{W_i}-1) < 2^{W_i+1}, and s + (carries<<1)... the identity s + 2·carries = a+b+c holds exactly (bitwise: for each bit position, the sum bit and carry). Yes, the 3:2 identity is exact: a + b + c = s + 2·c where c is the carry vector. So S_i + C_i = S' + C' + prd' exactly, PROVIDED no truncation (C_i has W_i bits; the top carry out of bit W_i-1 would be lost — the carry out of the MSB. Since a+b+c < 2^{W_i+1}, the carry out of the top position can be 1, and it's lost if C_i only has W_i bits).

So S_i + C_i = V_{i-1} + m·2^{W_{i-1}} + s·(2^{W_i} - 2^{W_{i-1}}) + prd' - (lost carry)·2^{W_i}.

We want S_i + C_i ≡ V_i (mod 2^{W_i}) where V_i = V_{i-1} + prd. The error terms: m·2^{W_{i-1}} + s·2^{W_i} - s·2^{W_{i-1}} - lost·2^{W_i}. The terms with 2^{W_i} vanish mod 2^{W_i}. The remaining error: (m - s)·2^{W_{i-1}}. So we need m = s for correctness!

m ∈ {0,1} is the "wrap" of the previous stage's sum, s is the sign bit of S_{i-1}. Is m = s always? Hmm. m = 1 iff S_{i-1} + C_{i-1} ≥ 2^{W_{i-1}}. s = the top bit of S_{i-1}. These aren't obviously equal.

Hmm. So the naive widening fails. BUT what if we choose the representation carefully? There's a classic trick: keep the CS pair such that C < 2^{W-1}... or use a "carry-save with restricted carry". Alternatively, note that we can ADD a correction term: the error (m-s)·2^{W_{i-1}} could be compensated if we knew m-s. Not easily.

How do I get Yosys to produce a CS multiplier? I can write it manually:
```
// a (8b signed) * w (8b signed) as a CS pair:
// partial products with Baugh-Wooley or sign-extension handling
```
Signed 8×8: a = -a7·2^7 + sum_{k<7} a_k 2^k; similarly w. The product = sum over 64 AND terms with signs. A clean way: use the "shift and add with sign extension" — the standard approach: treat both as unsigned with correction:
a×w (signed) = (a_u - 2^7·a7)(w_u - 2^7·w7) = a_u·w_u - 2^7·a7·w_u - 2^7·w7·a_u + 2^14·a7·w7.
So: compute the unsigned product a_u·w_u as a CS pair, then subtract/add the correction terms. The corrections: -2^7·a7·w_u (subtract w_u shifted by 7 if a7) and -2^7·w7·a_u, +2^14·a7·w7. These can be folded into the partial product array (Baugh-Wooley style: complement the sign partial products and add correction constants).

Simplest robust approach: build the partial product array explicitly with Baugh-Wooley:
For signed a×w (n=8): PP[i][j] = a_i & w_j for i,j < 7; the sign terms: for i=7 (a's sign) or j=7 (w's sign), the PP is complemented (negated) with a correction of +1 at the appropriate weight, plus the constant 2^n (the a7·w7 term becomes positive).

Baugh-Wooley for 8×8 signed:
product = Σ_{i=0..6, j=0..6} a_i w_j 2^{i+j}
        - Σ_{i=0..6} a_i w_7 2^{i+7}
        - Σ_{j=0..6} a_7 w_j 2^{7+j}
        + a_7 w_7 2^{14}
The negative terms are handled by inverting the PP bits and adding 1s at weight 2^{i+7} etc. Standard: 
- For the term -a_i w_7 2^{i+7}: use ~(a_i & w_7) at weight 2^{i+7} and add a correction 1 at weight 2^{i+7}... hmm, the classic Baugh-Wooley adds a constant 1 at bit position n (2^7) for each of the two negative groups... Let me just do it more simply:

**Simplest correct approach**: compute the product as an unsigned multiplication of magnitudes? No, sign handling.

**Alternative: let Yosys do the signed mul but capture it in CS form**: not directly expressible.

**Alternative: use the "add the correction terms into the chain" trick**: The product = a_u·w_u - 2^7·(a7·w_u) - 2^7·(w7·a_u) + 2^14·(a7·w7). Note a7·w_u = w_u if a7 else 0 = the "conditional negate". Hmm, this is getting messy but it's mechanical. Actually, here's a cleaner way to think: 

**Use the identity: signed product = unsigned product of (a xor sign-extension)... ** hmm.

Actually, the CLEANEST: build a 9×9 unsigned-ish array where both operands are sign-extended to 9 bits hmm, that gives an 18-bit product with the top bits redundant.

Hmm, let me think again: sign-extend a to 9 bits (a8 = a7) and w to 9 bits (w8 = w7). Then the 9×9 SIGNED product = the 8×8 signed product ✓ (sign-extension doesn't change the value). The 9×9 signed product's partial products: PP[i][j] = a_i & w_j for i,j ≤ 7, with the last row/column negated... same issue.

OK, Baugh-Wooley properly. For an n×n signed multiply (n=8), the partial product array:
- Rows i=0..n-2 (a_i, i < 7): PP[i][j] = a_i & w_j for j=0..n-2, and PP[i][n-1] = ~(a_i & w_{n-1}) [inverted].
- Row i=n-1 (a_7): PP[7][j] = ~(a_7 & w_j) for j=0..n-2, and PP[7][7] = a_7 & w_7 [positive].
- Corrections: add 1 at bit position n-1... The standard Baugh-Wooley correction: add a constant 1 at bit n (for the inverted column terms) — specifically, the sum of the inverted terms needs +1 per inverted term at its weight... 

The rule: -x = ~x + 1. So each inverted PP term ~(a_i&w_{7}) at weight 2^{i+7} represents -(a_i&w_7)·2^{i+7} + (2^{i+7} - 1)... hmm, ~t = (2^{i+7+1} - 1) - t when t is a single bit at weight i+7? Let me be careful: if t ∈ {0,1} is placed at bit position p, then ~t placed at bit position p (as a single bit) equals (1-t)·2^p = 2^p - t·2^p. So using ~t instead of t contributes +2^p extra. So the total correction = Σ over inverted terms of 2^p. For Baugh-Wooley 8×8: the inverted terms are: (i,7) for i=0..6 at weight 2^{i+7}, and (7,j) for j=0..6 at weight 2^{7+j}.   -> create_file(content="// Optimized TPU MXU clone (cycle-exact vs logikbench tpu).\n//\n// Key optimization vs a plain PE mesh: each cell's multiplier is written as an\n// explicit Baugh-Wooley partial-product array reduced by 3:2 compressors to a\n// CARRY-SAVE pair (no internal carry-propagate adder), and the column\n// partial-sum chain compresses (S, C, ms, mc) with two more 3:2 levels. .cy(c1));\n   cs3 u2 (.a(r3), .b(r4), .c(r5), .s(s2), .cy(c2));\n   cs3 u3 (.a(s1), .b(c1), .c(s2), .s(s3), .cy(c3));\n   cs3 u4 (.a(c2), .b(r6), .c(r7), .s(s4), .cy(c4));\n   cs3 u5 (.a(s3), .b(c3), [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n   );\n\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: per-column south shift while w_valid.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if Now let me fuse the multiplier's partial-product tree with the chain compression into a single 5-level tree (instead of 4+2=6 levels), entering the late-arriving S/C at the shallow levels:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized TPU MXU clone (cycle-exact vs logikbench tpu).\n//\n// Structure: per-cell Baugh-Wooley partial-product arrays are reduced\n// TOGETHER with the column's carry-save accumulator state in one balanced\n// 5-level 3:2 tree (10 rows -> 2), so no carry-propagate adder and no\n// separate multiplier adder is on the critical path.  The Baugh-Wooley array\n// sums to (product + 32512); eight products are accumulated per column, so a\n// constant is subtracted at the final (off-path) adder (mod 2^19).\nmodule cs3\n  (\n   input [18:0]\ta,\n   input [18:0]\tb,\n   input [18:0]\tc,\n   output [18:0] s,\n   output [18:0] cy\n   );\n   assign s\t = a ^ b ^ c;\n   assign cy\t = {((a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c)), 1'b0};\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t     clk,\n    input\t\t     rst,\n    input\t\t     w_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     w_data,\n    input\t\t     a_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\t     a_data,\n    output\t\t     c_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]     c_data\n   );\n\n   wire en = ~w_valid;\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Weight storage: per-column south shift while w_valid.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] w [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   integer       wi, wj;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (w_valid) begin\n         for (wj = 0; wj < N; wj = wj + 1) begin\n            w[0][wj] <= w_data[wj*DW +: DW];\n            for (wi = 1; wi < N; wi = wi + 1)\n              w[wi][wj] <= w[wi-1][wj];\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Activation skew + east flow.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:N-1][0:N+5];\n   integer       si, sk;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (en) begin\n         for (si = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_taprow\n         for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_tapcol\n            if (gi == 0 && gj == 0) begin : g_c00\n               assign tap[0][0] = a_valid ? a_data[0 +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n            end\n            else begin : g_cnn\n               assign tap[gi][gj] = sr[gi][gi+gj-1];\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   //##########################################################################\n   // Carry-save partial-sum chain with fused multipliers.\n   //##########################################################################\n   reg [18:0] S [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   reg [18:0] C [0:N-1][0:N-1];\n   wire [18:0] colres [0:N-1];\n\n   generate\n      for (gj = 0; gj < N; gj = gj + 1) begin : g_col\n         for (gi = 0; gi < N; gi = gi + 1) begin : g_row\n            // Baugh-Wooley rows for this cell: sum = a*w + 32512\n            wire [7:0] aa = tap[gi][gj];\n            wire [7:0] ww = w[gi][gj];\n            wire [7:0]  rb0 = {~(aa[7] & ww[0]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[0]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb1 = {~(aa[7] & ww[1]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[1]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb2 = {~(aa[7] & ww[2]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[2]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb3 = {~(aa[7] & ww[3]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[3]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb4 = {~(aa[7] & ww[4]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[4]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb5 = {~(aa[7] & ww[5]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[5]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb6 = {~(aa[7] & ww[6]), aa[6:0] & {7{ww[6]}}};\n            wire [7:0]  rb7 = {(aa[7] & ww[7]), ~(aa[6:0] & {7{ww[7]}})};\n            wire [18:0] r0  = {11'b0, rb0};\n            wire [18:0] r1  = {11'b0, rb1} << 1;\n            wire [18:0] r2  = {11'b0, rb2} << 2;\n            wire [18:0] r3  = {11'b0, rb3} << 3;\n            wire [18:0] r4  = {11'b0, rb4} << 4;\n            wire [18:0] r5  = {11'b0, rb5} << 5;\n            wire [18:0] r6  = {11'b0, rb6} << 6;\n            wire [18:0] r7  = {11'b0, rb7} << 7;\n\n            if (gi == 0) begin : g_first\n               // 8 rows -> 2 in 4 levels\n               wire [18:0] s1, c1, s2, c2, s3, c3, s4, c4, s5, c5;\n               cs3 u1 (.a(r0), .b(r1), .c(r2), .s(s1), .cy(c1));\n               cs3 u2 (.a(r3), .b(r4), .c(r5), .s(s2), .cy(c2));\n               cs3 u3 (.a(r6), .b(r7), .c(s1), .s(s3), .cy(c3));\n               cs3 u4 (.a(c1), .b(s2), .c(c2), .s(s4), .cy(c4));\n               cs3 u5 (.a(s3), .b(c3), .c(s4), .s(s5), .cy(c5));\n               cs3 u6 (.a(s5), .b(c5), .c(c4), .s(S[0][gj]), .cy(C[0][gj]));\n            end\n            else begin : g_rest\n               // (S,C) + 8 rows -> 2 in 5 levels; dj < N-1; dj = dj + 1) begin : g_dcol\n         localparam integer D = N-1-dj;\n         for (dk = 0; dk < D; dk = dk + 1) begin : g_dstage\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (en)\n                 dsr[dj][dk] <= (dk == 0) ? colres[dj] : dsr[dj][dk-1];\n            end\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   genvar oj;\n   generate\n      for (oj = 0; ## Summary

### Final result
**`design7.v`**: 2,043,832 ADP (area 4074, delay 501.7 ps), 100% correct on all 6782 cycle checks. This beats the starting point (4,624,409) by **2.26Ã**.

### What worked (in order of impact)

1. **Replace the PE mesh with a flat carry-save datapath** (`design3.v` â `design4.v`, 4.62M â 2.91M): Keep the systolic *timing* (taps at delay `i+j`, per-column accumulation, output de-skew `7-j`) but drop the module hierarchy; taper widths to 19 bits (results need only Â±8Â·128Â²); replace per-cell carry-propagate adders with 3:2 carry-save compressors plus one CPA per column off the critical path.

2. **Hand-built Baugh-Wooley carry-save multiplier** (`design6.v`, 2.91M â 2.09M): The single biggest win. Yosys's `a*b` maps to a partial-product tree **plus a ripple-carry adder on the critical path**. Writing the multiplier as an explicit Baugh-Wooley partial-product array reduced by 3:2 compressors to a (sum, carry) pair removes that ripple entirely. The array's constant offset (+32512/product) is subtracted once at the final off-path adder (8Ã32512, mod 2Â¹â¹). This dropped delay 670â520 ps while also cutting area.

3. **Balanced Wallace tree + fusing the multiplier tree with the chain compression** (`design7.v`, 2.09M â 2.04M): Instead of multiplier-tree (4 levels) then a separate 2-level chain compression, feed the incoming `S,C` state directly into the multiplier's reduction tree (10 rows â 2 in 5 levels), entering the register-sourced `S,C` at the shallow levels so the late-arriving FF outputs get the shortest logic depth.

### What failed / regressed

- **"Compute products later" restructures** (delay line + parallel per-column dot product, `design2.v`): broken by the **mid-drain weight reload** semantics â each product must use the weight value current at its exact original cycle. This pinning forbids almost all dataflow re-timing.
- **Tapered carry-save widths**: the 3:2 identity `S+C â¡ value` requires a **fixed modulus** at every stage; widening loses the wrap multiple.
- **Removing the enable mux via "recompress with zero products"**: fails because during `w_valid` the weights shift, so recompressing with the *new* weights corrupts in-flight sums.
- **Hierarchical references into generate blocks** (`g_col[j].res`) and undriven array elements: these synthesized to `x` in the netlist and failed netlist sim despite passing RTL sim.

### Lessons
- The critical structural insight was that only the *product cycle* is pinned (weight-snapshot semantics); everything else (representation, adder structure, tree shape) is free.
- Yosys's inferred multiplier/adder carry-propagate logic is the delay bottleneck; replacing it with explicit CSA trees was worth ~170 ps at no area cost.
- Register enable muxes (`if (en)`) sit on every critical path â a real but unrecoverable ~20-40 ps cost here given the reload semantics.
